TITLE: Quickstart: Running the DocumentApp Example
DESCRIPTION: This section provides quickstart instructions for installing the necessary Toga library and running the DocumentApp example. It covers basic execution and demonstrates how to open a specific document from the command line.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/examples/documentapp/README.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: bash
CODE:
```
pip install toga
```

LANGUAGE: bash
CODE:
```
python -m documentapp
```

LANGUAGE: bash
CODE:
```
python -m documentapp Jabberwocky.exampledoc
```

TITLE: Toga App Structure Example
DESCRIPTION: An example of a Toga application's startup method, including UI elements like text inputs and buttons, demonstrating how to initialize the application's interface.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/topics/file-management.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from pathlib import Path

import toga
from toga.style.pack import COLUMN


class ConfigFileCreator(toga.App):
    def startup(self):
        self.text_input = toga.MultilineTextInput()
        self.config_path_output = toga.TextInput(readonly=True)

        load_button = toga.Button(
            text="Load initial configuration",
            on_press=self.load_button_pressed,
            margin=20,
        )
        save_button = toga.Button(
            text="Save user configuration",
            on_press=self.save_button_pressed,
            margin=20,
        )

        main_box = toga.Box(
            direction=COLUMN,
            children=[
                self.text_input,
                self.config_path_output,
                load_button,
                save_button
            ],
        )
```

TITLE: Full Example: Tutorial 4 App
DESCRIPTION: This is the complete source code for the tutorial4 example application, demonstrating the usage of the Toga Canvas widget and various drawing contexts.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/tutorial-4.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from __future__ import annotations

import math

import toga
from toga.style import Pack
from toga.style.pack import COLUMN, ROW

WHITE = "#ffffff"


class Tutorial4(toga.App):
    def startup(self):
        # Main Window
        self.main_window = toga.MainWindow(title=self.formal_name)

        # Canvas
        self.canvas = toga.Canvas(flex=1)

        # Add the canvas to the main window
        box = toga.Box(children=[self.canvas])
        self.main_window.content = box

        # Draw the eyes
        self.draw_eyes()

        # Show the main window
        self.main_window.show()

    def draw_eyes(self):
        # Draw the whites of the eyes
        with self.canvas.fill(color=WHITE) as eye_whites:
            eye_whites.arc(58, 92, 15)
            eye_whites.arc(88, 92, 15, math.pi, 3 * math.pi)

        # Draw the irises
        with self.canvas.fill(color="black") as irises:
            irises.arc(58, 92, 7)
            irises.arc(88, 92, 7, math.pi, 3 * math.pi)

        # Draw the pupils
        with self.canvas.fill(color="blue") as pupils:
            pupils.arc(58, 92, 3)
            pupils.arc(88, 92, 3, math.pi, 3 * math.pi)

        # Draw the mouth
        with self.canvas.stroke(color="black", width=3) as mouth:
            mouth.arc(73, 115, 20, 0, math.pi)


def main() -> Tutorial4:
    return Tutorial4()

```

TITLE: WebView Usage Example
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to create and use a Toga WebView widget, including setting a URL and loading HTML content.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/webview.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

webview = toga.WebView()

# Request a URL be loaded in the webview.
webview.url = "https://beeware.org"

# Load a URL, and wait (non-blocking) for the page to complete loading
await webview.load_url("https://beeware.org")

# Load static HTML content into the wevbiew.
webview.set_content("https://example.com", "<html>...</html>")
```

TITLE: Basic App Initialization and Main Loop
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates the simplest way to create and run a Toga application. It initializes the App with a name and ID, then starts the main event loop.

TITLE: Fahrenheit to Celsius Converter Example
DESCRIPTION: This Python code implements a simple Fahrenheit to Celsius converter application using the Toga GUI toolkit. It demonstrates basic widget creation (Labels, TextInputs, Buttons) and layout management with Toga's Pack style engine, including vertical stacking and horizontal arrangement of widgets. The TextInput widgets are configured with flex=1 to utilize available space.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/tutorial-1.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga.style import Pack
from toga.style.pack import COLUMN, ROW
from toga.colors import RED
from toga.fonts import FONT_SIZE_LARGE

class Tutorial1(App):

    def startup(self):
        # Main window
        main_window = Window(title='Tutorial 1')

        # Widgets
        label1 = Label(
            'Fahrenheit',
            style=Pack(padding=(0, 5))
        )
        self.fahrenheit_input = TextInput(style=Pack(flex=1))

        label2 = Label(
            'Celsius',
            style=Pack(padding=(0, 5))
        )
        self.celsius_input = TextInput(style=Pack(flex=1))

        button = Button(
            'Convert',
            on_press=self.convert_temperature,
            style=Pack(padding=5, background_color=RED, color='white')
        )

        # Layout
        # Outer box
        outer_box = Box(
            children=[
                label1,
                self.fahrenheit_input,
                label2,
                self.celsius_input,
                button
            ],
            style=Pack(direction=COLUMN, padding=10, background_color='#f0f0f0')
        )

        # Add the outer box to the main window
        main_window.content = outer_box

        # Show the main window
        main_window.show()

    def convert_temperature(self, widget):
        try:
            fahrenheit = float(self.fahrenheit_input.value)
            celsius = (fahrenheit - 32) * 5 / 9
            self.celsius_input.value = str(celsius)
        except ValueError:
            self.celsius_input.value = 'Invalid input'


def main():
    return Tutorial1()

```

TITLE: Toga Button Widget Example
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates the usage of the Toga Button widget, including simple buttons with callbacks, disabled buttons, and buttons with different styles.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/examples/button/README.md#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga
from toga.style import Pack
from toga.style.pack import COLUMN, ROW

class ButtonExample(toga.App):

    def on_button_click(self, widget):
        print("Button clicked!")

    def startup(self):
        main_box = toga.Box(style=Pack(direction=COLUMN, padding=10))

        # Simple button with a callback
        simple_button = toga.Button(
            'Click Me',
            on_press=self.on_button_click,
            style=Pack(width=200, height=50)
        )
        main_box.add(simple_button)

        # Disabled button
        disabled_button = toga.Button(
            'Disabled Button',
            enabled=False,
            style=Pack(width=200, height=50)
        )
        main_box.add(disabled_button)

        # Button with different style (e.g., secondary)
        secondary_button = toga.Button(
            'Secondary Button',
            style=Pack(width=200, height=50, background_color='#f0f0f0', color='#333')
        )
        main_box.add(secondary_button)

        self.main_window = toga.MainWindow(title=self.formal_name)
        self.main_window.content = main_box
        self.main_window.show()


def main():
    return ButtonExample('Button Example', 'org.beeware.widgets.button')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main().main_loop()
```

TITLE: Toga App Tutorial 2 Example
DESCRIPTION: This Python code demonstrates a Toga application layout using CSS-like styling for widgets. It showcases the use of Table, SplitContainer, and ScrollContainer, along with window toolbars and a custom App subclass with a startup() method.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/tutorial-2.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from __future__ import annotations

import os
import sys

from typing import Any

import toga
from toga.style import Pack
from toga.style.parse import parse


class TutorialApp(toga.App):
    def startup(self) -> None:
        # Main window
        main_window = toga.MainWindow(title=self.formal_name)

        # Split container
        split_container = toga.SplitContainer()

        # Left pane: Table
        table = toga.Table(
            headings=['Col 1', 'Col 2'],
            data=[('Row 1', 'Data 1'), ('Row 2', 'Data 2')],
            style=Pack(flex=1)
        )
        split_container.content = table

        # Right pane: Scroll container with a button
        button = toga.Button(
            'A Button',
            on_press=self.on_button_press,
            style=Pack(padding=10, width=100)
        )
        scroll_container = toga.ScrollContainer(
            content=toga.Box(children=[button], style=Pack(direction='column', padding=20, height=200)),
            style=Pack(flex=1)
        )
        split_container.sash_gravity = [(1, 0.3)]
        split_container.content = split_container

        # Add split container to main window
        main_window.content = split_container

        # Add toolbar
        main_window.toolbar.add(
            toga.ToolbarDivider(),
            toga.ToolbarButton(
                'Action 1',
                icon='resources/icons/icon-red.png',
                on_press=self.on_toolbar_button_press,
            ),
            toga.ToolbarButton(
                'Action 2',
                icon='resources/icons/icon-blue.png',
                on_press=self.on_toolbar_button_press,
            ),
        )

        # Show the main window
        main_window.show()

    def on_button_press(self, widget: toga.Button) -> None:
        print('Button pressed!')

    def on_toolbar_button_press(self, widget: toga.ToolbarButton) -> None:
        print(f"Toolbar button '{widget.text}' pressed")


def main() -> int:
    return TutorialApp().main_loop()


if __name__ == '__main__':
    sys.exit(main())

```

TITLE: Toga Slider Usage Example

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

def my_callback(slider):
    print(slider.value)

# Continuous slider, with an event handler.
toga.Slider(min=-5, max=10, value=7, on_change=my_callback)

# Discrete slider, accepting the values [0, 1.5, 3, 4.5, 6, 7.5].
toga.Slider(min=0, max=7.5, tick_count=6)
```

TITLE: Button Widget Example
DESCRIPTION: Illustrates the three-layer architecture using the Toga Button widget, showing how the public API, platform implementation, and native widget interact.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/internals/architecture.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: text
CODE:
```
Interface Layer:
  - `toga.Button` defined in `core/src/toga/widgets/button.py`.
  - Defines the public interface, including `on_click` event handler.

Implementation Layer:
  - `toga-gtk.widgets.Button` defined in `gtk/src/toga-gtk/widgets/button.py`.
  - Defines the GTK implementation, connecting GTK's `clicked` signal to Toga's `on_click` handler.

Native Layer:
  - `Gtk.Button` is the native GTK-Python widget.

This layered approach allows changing implementations (e.g., `toga-gtk` to `toga-cocoa`) without altering the public API.
```

TITLE: Example Document Implementation
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to create a custom Toga Document subclass, defining its description, file extensions, and implementing methods for creating the main window, reading file content, and writing content back to the file. The `touch` method is used to mark the document as modified when its content changes.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/document.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

class ExampleDocument(toga.Document):
    description = "Example Document"
    extensions = ["mydoc", "mydocument"]

    def create(self):
        # Create the main window for the document. The window has a single widget; 
        # when that widget changes, the document is modified.
        self.main_window = toga.DocumentMainWindow(
            doc=self,
            content=toga.MultilineTextInput(on_change=self.touch),
        )

    def read(self):
        # Read the content of the file represented by the document, and populate the
        # widgets in the main window with that content.
        with self.path.open() as f:
            self.main_window.content.value = f.read()

    def write(self):
        # Save the content currently displayed by the main window.
        with self.path.open("w") as f:
            f.write(self.main_window.content.value)
```

```
class MyApp(App):

        # The framework now checks if a main window is created here.
        # If not, it might raise an error or handle it gracefully.
        self.main_window = Window(title='My App')
        self.main_window.show()
```

TITLE: Toga Button Widget Layered API Example
DESCRIPTION: Illustrates how the Toga Button widget is structured across the three architectural layers (Interface, Implementation, Native) using the GTK platform as an example. It shows how the public API, platform-specific implementation, and native toolkit widget interact to provide cross-platform functionality.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/internals/architecture.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Interface Layer:
  toga.Button:
    Defined in: core/src/toga/widgets/button.py
    Purpose: Public interface for the Button widget.
    Attributes:
      on_click: Event handler for button clicks.
    Description: Defines the high-level concept of a button, independent of platform.

Implementation Layer (GTK):
  toga-gtk.widgets.Button:
    Defined in: gtk/src/toga-gtk/widgets/button.py
    Purpose: Platform-specific implementation for GTK.
    Description: Describes how to create a button on GTK and connects the native GTK 'clicked' signal to the Toga 'on_click' handler.

Native Layer (GTK):
  Gtk.Button:
    Purpose: The actual native GTK-Python widget API.
    Description: Provides the underlying button functionality on the GTK platform.
```

TITLE: Toga API Design Principles
DESCRIPTION: This section details the core principles guiding Toga's API design. It covers coding style, property usage, constructor patterns, and event handling mechanisms. The API aims for a Pythonic feel, using properties over getters/setters, allowing flexible constructor arguments, and employing a consistent event naming convention starting with 'on_'. Event handlers receive the widget and event-specific arguments, and should include **kwargs for forward compatibility.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/topics/api-design.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Coding Style:
  - Follows Pythonic idioms and naming conventions (snake_case).
  - US English spelling.

Properties:
  - Exposes object state via property notation (e.g., widget.property).
  - Adheres to Postel's Law: accepts any object when set, returns a string when retrieved (e.g., text property).

Constructors:
  - Writable properties can be initialized via keyword arguments.
  - Read-only properties (e.g., Widget.id) can also be initialized.
  - Single required arguments (e.g., Label text) can be positional.

Events:
  - Used for user actions, handlers assigned to properties starting with 'on_'.
  - Named for general purpose (e.g., on_press for Button).
  - Handlers receive widget as positional arg, event-specific info as keyword args.
  - Handlers should include **kwargs for forward compatibility.
  - Property changes trigger events unless the value remains the same.

Common Names:
  - Simple values: 'value' property, 'on_change' event (e.g., TextInput, Switch).
  - Non-editable captions: 'text' property (e.g., Button, Switch).
  - Number ranges: 'min' and 'max' properties.
```

----------------------------------------

TITLE: Toga Android App API
DESCRIPTION: Documentation for the `toga_android.App.start_activity` method, which allows starting native Android activities from a Toga application. It details the parameters for the activity intent and the callback function.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/platforms/android.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: apidoc
CODE:
```
toga_android.App.start_activity(self, activity, *options, on_complete=None)
  Start a native Android activity.
  Parameters:
    activity: The ``android.content.Intent`` instance to start
    options: Any additional arguments to pass to the native ``android.app.Activity.startActivityForResult()`` call.
    on_complete: A callback to invoke when the activity completes. The callback will be invoked with 2 arguments: the result code, and the result data.
```

TITLE: Load Configuration File
DESCRIPTION: Loads configuration content, first attempting to read from the user's config directory and then falling back to an initial resource file if the user config is not found. This ensures a default configuration is always available.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/topics/file-management.rst#_snippet_11

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
path = self.paths.config / "config.toml"
if not path.exists():
    path = self.paths.app / "resources/initial_config.toml"
self.text_input.value = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
```

LANGUAGE: python

```
# Core Toga API package
# Requires a backend for platform-specific implementation

# Available Backends:
# Android: toga-android
# iOS: toga-iOS
# Linux: toga-gtk
# macOS: toga-cocoa
# Textual: toga-textual
# Web: toga-web
# Windows: toga-winforms
```

TITLE: Using `__file__` for Absolute Path Construction
DESCRIPTION: This Python code demonstrates using the `__file__` attribute to construct an absolute path to the configuration file, making it more reliable.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/topics/file-management.rst#_snippet_6

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
def load_button_pressed(self, button, **kwargs):
    path = Path(__file__).parent / "resources/initial_config.toml"
    self.text_input.value = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
```

TITLE: Toga Layered Architecture Example
DESCRIPTION: Illustrates Toga's layered architecture, where a single widget interface abstracts platform-specific implementation details. This is exemplified by the construction of a DetailedList on macOS, which is built from multiple native components but presented as a unified widget to the developer.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/internals/architecture.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
class DetailedList:
    def __init__(self):
        # Interface layer widget
        pass

# Platform-specific implementation (e.g., macOS)
class MacOSDetailedList(DetailedList):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        # Implementation details using ScrollView, TableView, etc.
        pass

# Dummy implementation for testing
class DummyDetailedList(DetailedList):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__()
        # No graphical operations, just API satisfaction
        pass
```

```

    path.write_text(self.text_input.value, encoding="utf-8")
    self.config_path_output.value = path
```

TITLE: Continuous Location Tracking
DESCRIPTION: Details how to set up continuous location updates by defining an event handler and starting the tracking service. It also covers stopping the tracking and handling cases where permissions are not granted.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/hardware/location.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

class MyApp(toga.App):
    ...
    async def location_update(self, location, altitude, **kwargs):
        print(f"You are now at {location}, with altitude {altitude} meters")

    def start_location(self):
        # Install a location handler
        self.location.on_change = self.location_update
        # Start location updates. This assumes permissions have already been
        # requested and granted.
        try:
            self.location.start_tracking()
        except PermissionError:
            print("User has not permitted location tracking.")

    def stop_location_tracking(self):
        self.location.stop_tracking()
```

TITLE: Create Tree with Dictionary Data
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates creating a Toga Tree widget using a dictionary to represent hierarchical data. Each key-value pair in the dictionary represents a node, with values being either child dictionaries or None. This example shows populating the tree and accessing its data.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/tree.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

tree = toga.Tree(
    headings=["Name", "Age"],
    data={
        "Earth": {
           ("Arthur Dent", 42): None,
        },
        "Betelgeuse Five": {
           ("Ford Prefect", 37): None,
           ("Zaphod Beeblebrox", 47): None,
        },
    }
)

# Get the details of the first child of the second root node:
print(f"{tree.data[1][0].name} is age {tree.data[1][0].age}")

# Append new data to the first root node in the tree
tree.data[0].append(("Tricia McMillan", 38))
```

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:

```
from toga.style.pack import Pack, COLUMN

box = toga.Box(
    children=[
        toga.Label("First section"),
        toga.Divider(),
        toga.Label("Second section"),
    ],
    direction=COLUMN,
    flex=1,
    margin=10
)
```

TITLE: Toga Pack to CSS Property Mapping
DESCRIPTION: This table details the direct mapping of Toga Pack layout properties to their corresponding CSS properties. It serves as a guide for understanding how Toga's layout system translates to web standards.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/style/pack.rst#_snippet_6

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Toga Pack to CSS Property Mapping:

============================= ===================================================
Pack property                 CSS property
============================= ===================================================
``direction: <str>``          ``flex-direction: <str>``
``display: pack``             ``display: flex``
``flex: <int>``               If ``direction == "row"`` and ``width`` is set,
                                 or ``direction == "column"`` and ``height`` is set,
                                 ignore. Otherwise, ``flex: <int> 0 auto``.
``font_size: <int>``          ``font-size: <int>pt``
``height: <value>``           ``height: <value>px`` if value is an integer;
                                 ``height: auto`` if value is ``"none"``.
``margin_top: <int>``         ``margin-top: <int>px``
``margin_bottom: <int>``      ``margin-bottom: <int>px``
``margin_left: <int>``        ``margin-left: <int>px``
``margin_right: <int>``       ``margin-right: <int>px``
``text_direction: <str>``     ``direction: <str>``
``width: <value>``            ``width: <value>px`` if value is an integer;
                                 ``width: auto`` if value is ``"none"``.
============================= ===================================================

Note: All other Pack declarations should be used as-is as CSS declarations, with underscores converted to dashes (e.g., ``background_color`` becomes ``background-color``).
```

TITLE: Subclassing Toga.App for Complex Applications
DESCRIPTION: Illustrates creating a custom application by subclassing `toga.App`. The `startup` method is overridden to define the main window and its content, providing a structure for more complex applications.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/app.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

class MyApp(toga.App):
    def startup(self):
        self.main_window = toga.MainWindow()
        self.main_window.content = toga.Box(children=[toga.Label("Hello!")])
        self.main_window.show()

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app = MyApp("Realistic App", "org.beeware.realistic")
    app.main_loop()
```

TITLE: Basic Drawing with Toga Canvas
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to draw a simple orange horizontal line on a Toga Canvas using the context object. It shows the basic steps of creating a canvas, getting the context, beginning a path, moving to a point, drawing a line, and stroking with a color.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/canvas.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga
canvas = toga.Canvas()
context = canvas.context

context.begin_path()
context.move_to(20, 20)
context.line_to(160, 20)
context.stroke(color="orange")
```

TITLE: Deprecated add_background_task and New on_running Event Handler in Toga
DESCRIPTION: The `add_background_task()` API on `toga.App` has been deprecated. Background tasks should now be implemented using the new `on_running` event handler, which is triggered when the app starts running, or by directly utilizing `asyncio.create_task` for more general asynchronous operations.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/project/releases.rst#_snippet_12

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.App:
  add_background_task():
    Status: Deprecated
    Replacement: Use on_running event handler or asyncio.create_task.
  on_running():
    Description: New event handler for background tasks, invoked when the app starts running.
    Usage: Implement asynchronous operations or long-running tasks here.
```

LANGUAGE: Python
CODE:
```
import asyncio
import toga

class MyApp(toga.App):
    async def on_running(self):
        # Example of a background task using asyncio
        asyncio.create_task(self._my_background_task())

    async def _my_background_task(self):
        print("Background task started")
        await asyncio.sleep(5)
        print("Background task finished")

    def startup(self):
        self.main_window = toga.MainWindow(self.name)
        self.main_window.show()

# app = MyApp('My App', 'org.example.myapp')
# app.main_loop()
```

TITLE: Toga SplitContainer and Toolbar Tutorial
DESCRIPTION: This tutorial explores the SplitContainer widget for displaying multiple components, including toolbars and tables, enabling dynamic resizing of content areas.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/index.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga import App, SplitContainer, Toolbar, Table, Box

class SplitApp(App):
    def startup(self):
        toolbar = Toolbar(items=[('Action 1', self.action1), ('Action 2', self.action2)])
        table = Table(headings=['Column 1', 'Column 2'], data=[('Row 1 Col 1', 'Row 1 Col 2')])
        
        # Example of using SplitContainer
        split_container = SplitContainer(
            content=(toolbar, table)
        )
        
        self.main_window.content = split_container

    def action1(self, widget):
        print('Action 1 triggered')

    def action2(self, widget):
        print('Action 2 triggered')

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app = SplitApp()
    app.main_loop()

```

TITLE: Toga Layout Properties: Padding/Alignment Renamed
DESCRIPTION: The `padding` and `alignment` properties for Toga's layout system have been renamed to `margin` and `align_items` respectively, to align with CSS terminology. `align_items` now accepts CSS-compatible values (`START`, `CENTER`, `END`) instead of the previous `TOP`/`RIGHT`/`BOTTOM`/`LEFT`/`CENTER`. The old names are deprecated but still functional.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/about/releases.rst#_snippet_34

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
APIDOC:
  Layout Properties: Renaming of padding and alignment

Description:
  Layout properties `padding` and `alignment` have been renamed to `margin` and `align_items` to match CSS standards.

Details:
  - `padding` is now `margin`.
  - `alignment` is now `align_items`.
  - `align_items` accepts new values: `START`, `CENTER`, `END` (CSS-compatible).
  - Old property names (`padding`, `alignment`) and `alignment` values are deprecated but still supported.

Backward Incompatible Changes:
  - Code using `padding` and `alignment` should be updated to use `margin` and `align_items` for future compatibility.
```

----------------------------------------

TITLE: Rename Pack Layout Properties padding and alignment
DESCRIPTION: Documents the renaming of `Pack` layout properties `padding` to `margin` and `alignment` to `align_items` for CSS compatibility. It also specifies the new CSS-compatible values (`START`, `CENTER`, `END`) for `align_items`, while noting the old names and values are deprecated but still functional.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/project/releases.rst#_snippet_5

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Pack:
  padding:
    Deprecated: Use `margin`.
    Type: Length
  margin:
    Purpose: Defines the outer spacing of the widget (CSS analogue).
    Type: Length
  alignment:
    Deprecated: Use `align_items`.
    Values: TOP, RIGHT, BOTTOM, LEFT, CENTER
  align_items:
    Purpose: Defines how items are aligned along the cross axis (CSS analogue).
    Values: START, CENTER, END
```

TITLE: TreeSource API Changes
DESCRIPTION: The `parent` argument has been removed from `TreeSource.insert` and `TreeSource.append`. To insert or append rows to a descendant of a TreeSource root, use the `insert` and `append` methods on the parent node itself. For example, `source.insert(parent, index, ...)` becomes `parent.insert(index, ...)` and `source.insert(None, index, ...)` becomes `source.insert(index, ...)`.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/about/releases.rst#_snippet_186

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
source.insert(parent, index, ...)
# becomes
parent.insert(index, ...)

source.insert(None, index, ...)
# becomes
source.insert(index, ...)
```

TITLE: Hello World App
DESCRIPTION: Python code for a simple Toga application featuring a button that prints 'hello' to the console when pressed.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/tutorial-0.rst#_snippet_3

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

def button_handler(widget):
    print("hello")

def build(app):
    box = toga.Box()
    button = toga.Button("Hello world", on_press=button_handler)
    button.style.margin = 50
    button.style.flex = 1
    box.add(button)
    return box

app = toga.App("Hello World", "org.example.helloworld", app_module='helloworld')
app.main_window.content = build(app)
app.main_loop()
```

TITLE: ListSource Initialization and Usage
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to create a ListSource with specified accessors and data, and perform basic operations like accessing, finding, removing, and inserting items.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/sources/list_source.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga.sources import ListSource

source = ListSource(
    accessors=["name", "weight"],
    data=[
        {"name": "Platypus", "weight": 2.4},
        {"name": "Numbat", "weight": 0.597},
        {"name": "Thylacine", "weight": 30.0},
    ]
)

# Get the first item in the source
item = source[0]
print(f"Animal's name is {item.name}")

# Find an item with a name of "Thylacine"
item = source.find({"name": "Thylacine"})

# Remove that item from the data
source.remove(item)

# Insert a new item at the start of the data
source.insert(0, {"name": "Bettong", "weight": 1.2})
```

```
stuff_group = toga.Group('Stuff', order=40)

    callback,
    text='Example command',
    tooltip='Tells you when it has been activated',

)
```

TITLE: Toga Window API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides an overview of the Toga Window class and related protocols, including initialization, content management, and event handling.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/window.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Window:
  __init__(title=None, position=(0, 0), size=None, minimizable=True, resizable=True, closeable=True, on_close=None, content=None)
    Initializes a new Window.
    Parameters:
      title (str, optional): The title of the window. Defaults to None.
      position (tuple, optional): The initial position of the window (x, y). Defaults to (0, 0).
      size (tuple, optional): The initial size of the window (width, height). Defaults to None.
      minimizable (bool, optional): Whether the window can be minimized. Defaults to True.
      resizable (bool, optional): Whether the window can be resized. Defaults to True.
      closeable (bool, optional): Whether the window can be closed. Defaults to True.
      on_close (callable, optional): A handler for the window close event. Defaults to None.
      content (toga.Widget, optional): The initial content of the window. Defaults to None.

  show():
    Makes the window visible.

  close():
    Closes the window. Once closed, a window cannot be reused.

  content:
    The main widget content of the window. Can be reassigned to change the window's content.

  on_close:
    A handler that is called when the user attempts to close the window. It must return a boolean indicating if the close is permitted.

toga.app.WindowSet:
  Represents a collection of windows managed by the application.

toga.window.Dialog:
  Protocol for defining dialogs within a window.

toga.window.OnCloseHandler:
  Type alias for the on_close event handler.

toga.window.DialogResultHandler:
  Protocol for handling the result of a dialog.
```

TITLE: Creating Toga Images
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates various methods for creating a Toga Image object, including loading from files, raw data, and PIL Images.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/images.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from pathlib import Path
import toga

# Load an image in the same folder as the file that declares the App class
my_image = toga.Image("brutus.png")

# Load an image at an absolute path
my_image = toga.Image(Path.home() / "path/to/brutus.png")

# Create an image from raw data
with (Path.home() / "path/to/brutus.png").open("rb") as f:
    my_image = toga.Image(data=f.read())

# Create an image from a PIL image (if PIL is installed)
import PIL.Image
my_pil_image = PIL.Image.new("L", (30, 30))
my_toga_image = toga.Image(my_pil_image)
```

TITLE: Implementing a New Platform Backend for Toga
DESCRIPTION: Steps for creating a new platform backend for Toga, focusing on integrating with the event loop, positioning widgets, and implementing core classes like Application and Window.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/how-to/contribute/code.rst#_snippet_17

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Implement an entirely new platform backend

Create a new backend for Toga to support additional platforms.

Initial Steps:
1. Implement Toga Application and Window classes to create an empty window integrated with the Python `asyncio` event loop.
2. Use native platform APIs to position widgets, replacing native layout schemes with Toga's layout algorithm. Focus on placing a button with a fixed size at a specific position.
3. Get Tutorial 0 working: Implement the factory class for the new backend and connect Toga style applicator methods to set widget positions.

Subsequent Steps:
- Implement widgets and other Toga features (fonts, images, etc.).

Example Target:
- Qt-based backend for KDE-based Linux desktops (See issue #1142).
```

```
toga-winforms
=============

|pyversions| |license| |maturity|

A Microsoft .NET backend for the `Toga widget toolkit`_ utilizing the WinForms API.
```

TITLE: Toga Web Browser App
DESCRIPTION: A Python script for a basic web browser using Toga. It includes a text input for URLs, a button to navigate, and a web view to display content. The application is structured as a class.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/tutorial/tutorial-3.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga.style import Pack
from toga.style.pack import COLUMN, ROW
from toga.colors import BLUE, RED
from toga.fonts import ITALIC, BOLD
from toga.constants import CENTER, MIDDLE

from toga import App, Window, Button, TextInput, WebView

class Browser(App):

    def startup(self):
        # Main window
        self.main_window = Window(title=self.formal_name)

        # URL input
        url_input = TextInput(
            label='URL:',
            initial='https://beeware.org',
            style=Pack(flex=1)
        )

        # Go button
        go_button = Button(
            'Go',
            on_press=self.go_to_url,
            style=Pack(padding=5, width=100)
        )

        # Web view
        self.web_view = WebView(
            url='https://beeware.org',
            style=Pack(flex=1)
        )

        # Layout
        self.main_window.content = Box(
            children=[
                Box(
                    children=[
                        url_input,
                        go_button
                    ],
                    style=Pack(direction=ROW, padding=10)
                ),
                self.web_view
            ],
            style=Pack(direction=COLUMN, padding=10)
        )

        self.main_window.show()

    def go_to_url(self, widget):
        url = widget.parent.children[0].value
        self.web_view.url = url


def main():
    return Browser('Browser', 'org.beeware.browser')

```

TITLE: toga.ProgressBar API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides the API documentation for the toga.ProgressBar class, detailing its constructor, attributes, and methods. This includes parameters for initialization and controlling the progress bar's state.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/progressbar.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
class toga.ProgressBar:
    __init__(max: int | None = None, value: int = 0, enabled: bool = True, style: Style | None = None, on_change: callable | None = None, **kwargs):
        """Initialize a ProgressBar.

        Args:
            max (int | None): The maximum value of the progress bar. If None, the progress bar is indeterminate.
            value (int): The current value of the progress bar. Ignored if the progress bar is indeterminate.
            enabled (bool): Is the widget enabled? Defaults to True.
            style (Style | None): An optional style object to contain this widget’s properties.
            on_change (callable | None): A function that will be called when the progress bar’s value changes.
            **kwargs: Variable keyword arguments.
        """
        pass

    def start():
        """Start the progress bar animation.
        """
        pass

    def stop():
        """Stop the progress bar animation.
        """
        pass

    @property
    def max(self) -> int | None:
        """The maximum value of the progress bar.
        """
        pass

    @max.setter
    def max(self, value: int | None):
        """Set the maximum value of the progress bar.
        """
        pass

    @property
    def value(self) -> int:
        """The current value of the progress bar.
        """
        pass

    @value.setter
    def value(self, value: int):
        """Set the current value of the progress bar.
        """
        pass
```

TITLE: Toga Document create() Method Requirement
DESCRIPTION: Documents must now provide a `create()` method to instantiate a `main_window` instance. This change standardizes the process of creating the primary window associated with a document.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/about/releases.rst#_snippet_195

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga.documents import Document
from toga.window import Window

class MyDocument(Document):
    def __init__(self, path):
        super().__init__(path=path)

    def create(self):
        # Instantiate and return the main window for this document
        return Window(title=str(self.path), main_window=True)
```

TITLE: Toga TextInput Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides API documentation for the Toga TextInput class, including its initialization, properties, and event handlers. This reference details the available methods and protocols for interacting with TextInput widgets.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/textinput.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.TextInput:
  __init__(id=None, style=None, factory=None, ...) 
    Initializes a new TextInput widget.
    Parameters:
      id (str, optional): A unique identifier for the widget.
      style (Style, optional): A Toga style object to apply to the widget.
      factory (callable, optional): A factory function to create the widget.

  value (str): The current text content of the input.

  placeholder (str): Text displayed when the input is empty, guiding the user.

  validators (list): A list of validator functions to check input validity.

  on_change (callable): Event handler for when the input value changes.

  on_confirm (callable): Event handler for when the user confirms input (e.g., presses Enter).

  on_gain_focus (callable): Event handler for when the widget receives focus.

  on_lose_focus (callable): Event handler for when the widget loses focus.

Protocols:
  toga.widgets.textinput.OnChangeHandler
  toga.widgets.textinput.OnConfirmHandler
  toga.widgets.textinput.OnGainFocusHandler
  toga.widgets.textinput.OnLoseFocusHandler

Notes:
  - Validation errors may not always be displayed to the user.
  - Winforms has limitations with transparent backgrounds and explicit height settings for TextInput.

```

TITLE: Widget Implementation Creation via _create() Method
DESCRIPTION: Details the new requirement for custom widgets to create and return their backend implementation via a `_create()` method. It clarifies the impact on existing code, noting that assigning implementation before `super().__init__()` will issue a warning, while assigning afterward will now raise an exception.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/project/releases.rst#_snippet_4

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Widget:
  _create():
    Purpose: Creates and returns the backend implementation for the widget.
    Usage: Custom widgets inheriting from Toga widgets should implement this method to specify their own backend.
    Impact:
      - Assigning implementation before `super().__init__()` will issue a `RuntimeWarning`.
      - Assigning implementation after `super().__init__()` will now raise an exception.
```

```
  Description: A free entry text field that provides options (e.g., text with past choices)
```

TITLE: Toga API Documentation
DESCRIPTION: API reference for key Toga classes related to commands, including Command, Group, CommandSet, Separator, and the ActionHandler protocol.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/command.rst#_snippet_4

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Command:
  __init__(self, command, text, tooltip=None, icon=None, group=None, order=0, enabled=True, action=None)
    Represents a command that can be invoked by the user.
    Parameters:
      command: The internal identifier for the command.
      text: The text label for the command.
      tooltip: Tooltip text for the command.
      icon: An icon for the command.
      group: The group the command belongs to.
      order: The order of the command within its group.
      enabled: Whether the command is currently enabled.
      action: The handler for the command.

  standard(cls, owner, command, **kwargs)
    Factory method to create a standard command.
    Parameters:
      owner: The owner of the command.
      command: The standard command identifier.
      **kwargs: Additional arguments for command creation.

  PREFERENCES: Class attribute for the standard Preferences command.
  ABOUT: Class attribute for the standard About command.
  OPEN: Class attribute for the standard Open command.

toga.Group:
  __init__(self, name, order=0, parent=None)
    Represents a group of commands.
    Parameters:
      name: The name of the group.
      order: The order of the group.
      parent: The parent group.

toga.command.CommandSet:
  __init__(self, owner)
    Manages a collection of commands.
    Parameters:
      owner: The owner of the command set.

  add(self, command)
    Adds a command to the set.

  remove(self, command)
    Removes a command from the set.

  __getitem__(self, key)
    Retrieves a command by its key.

  __delitem__(self, key)
    Deletes a command by its key.

toga.command.Separator:
  __init__(self, group=None, order=0)
    Represents a visual separator between commands.
    Parameters:
      group: The group the separator belongs to.
      order: The order of the separator.

toga.command.ActionHandler:
  protocol ActionHandler:
    Defines the interface for command actions.
    Methods:
      action(self, widget, **kwargs)
        The method called when the command is invoked.
        Parameters:
          widget: The widget that triggered the command.
          **kwargs: Additional arguments.
```

TITLE: Toga Widget: Tree (Roadmap)
DESCRIPTION: Describes the planned 'Tree' widget, noting its partial implementation, similar approach to Table, and a specific limitation on Windows.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/project/roadmap.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Tree:
  Status: Partially implemented
  Description: As with Table, mobile platforms don't provide a native "Tree" widget; however, we could use a similar approach to Table.
  Windows Specifics: On Windows, the native widget doesn't provide support for more than one column. This means we either need to make a special case on Windows for a "simple" tree; or we need to form a composite widget that pairs the scrolling of a table with a tree.
```

TITLE: Toga MainWindow API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides details on the toga.MainWindow class, including its inheritance, attributes, and methods. This serves as a reference for developers using or extending the MainWindow functionality.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/mainwindow.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
class toga.MainWindow(toga.Window)
  A window that can use the full set of window-level user interface elements.

  Inherits from: toga.Window

  Attributes:
    toolbar (list): A list of tool items that can be added to the window's toolbar.

  Usage:
    A :class:`toga.MainWindow` is a :class:`toga.Window` that can serve as the main
    interface to an application. A :class:`toga.MainWindow` may optionally have a toolbar.
    The presentation of :class:`toga.MainWindow` is platform dependent:

    * On desktop platforms that place menus inside windows (e.g., Windows, and most Linux
      window managers), a :class:`toga.MainWindow` instance will display a menu bar that
      contains the defined app :attr:`~toga.App.commands`.

    * On desktop platforms that use an app-level menu bar (e.g., macOS, and some Linux
      window managers), the window will not have a menu bar; all menu items will be
      displayed in the app bar.

    * On mobile, web and console platforms, a :class:`toga.MainWindow` will include a title
      bar that can contain both menus and toolbar items.

    Toolbar items can be added by adding them to :attr:`~toga.MainWindow.toolbar`; any
    command added to the toolbar will be automatically added to the App's commands as well.
```

TITLE: Basic Selection with Strings
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates the basic instantiation of a Toga Selection widget using a list of strings. Shows how to set and retrieve the selected value.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/selection.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

selection = toga.Selection(items=["Alice", "Bob", "Charlie"])

# Change the selection to "Charlie"
selection.value = "Charlie"

# Which item is currently selected? This will print "Charlie"
print(f"Currently selected: {selection.value}")
```

```
toga.NumberInput:
  __init__(min=None, max=None, step=None, value=None, on_change=None, style=None, id=None, enabled=True, readonly=False, **kwargs)
    min: The minimum allowed value for the input.
    max: The maximum allowed value for the input.
    step: The increment/decrement step for the input.
    value: The initial value of the input. Can be Decimal, int, float, or str.
    on_change: A callback function to be invoked when the input value changes.
    style: A Toga Style object to customize the widget's appearance.
    id: A unique identifier for the widget.
    enabled: Whether the widget is enabled.
    readonly: Whether the widget is read-only.

  Properties:

  Data Types:
    Accepts Decimal, int, float, or str containing numbers.
    Always returns Decimal objects to ensure precision.
```

```
def on_key_press(widget, event):
    if event.key == toga.Key.SPACE:
        print("Spacebar pressed!")
    elif event.key == toga.Key.SHIFT:

```

TITLE: Toga Widget Implementation: _create() Method
DESCRIPTION: Widgets in Toga now create and return their implementations via a `_create()` method. Custom widgets inheriting from existing Toga widgets with the same implementation require no changes. Custom widgets needing a specific implementation should define it in `_create()`. Existing code assigning implementation before `super().__init__()` will warn; implementation after `super().__init__()` will raise an exception.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/about/releases.rst#_snippet_33

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
APIDOC:
  Widget Implementation Creation: _create() Method

Description:
  Widgets now use a `_create()` method to instantiate their platform-specific implementations.

Details:
  - Custom widgets inheriting from Toga widgets with identical implementations are unaffected.
  - Custom widgets requiring unique implementations must define them within `_create()`.

Backward Incompatible Changes:
  - User code assigning implementation before `super().__init__()` will raise a `RuntimeWarning`.
  - User code assigning implementation after `super().__init__()` will raise an exception, breaking existing functionality.
```

```
class toga.DetailedList:
    def __init__(self, data=None, accessors=None, factory=None, style=None, 
                 missing_value='-', on_select=None, on_activate=None, 
                 on_primary_action=None, on_secondary_action=None, 
                 on_refresh=None, **kwargs):
        """Construct a new DetailedList widget.

        Args:
            data (list): A list of data items to display. Each item is typically a dictionary.
            accessors (tuple): A tuple of strings, specifying the keys to use for icon, title, and subtitle.
                               Defaults to ('icon', 'title', 'subtitle').
            factory (:module:`toga.frontend`): The factory to use for creating the widget.
            style (:module:`toga.Style`): An optional style object to apply to the widget.
            missing_value (str): The value to display if an accessor is missing or None.
            on_select (callable): A function to be called when an item is selected.
            on_activate (callable): A function to be called when an item is activated (e.g., double-clicked).
            on_primary_action (callable): A function to be called when the primary action is triggered.
            on_secondary_action (callable): A function to be called when the secondary action is triggered.
            on_refresh (callable): A function to be called when a refresh action is triggered.
            **kwargs: Arbitrary keyword arguments.
        """
        pass

    def refresh(self):
        """Refresh the content of the DetailedList."""
        pass

    def scroll_to_top(self):
        """Scroll the DetailedList to the top."""
        pass

    def scroll_to_index(self, index):
        """Scroll the DetailedList to a specific item index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index of the item to scroll to.
        """
        pass

    def scroll_to_item(self, item):
        """Scroll the DetailedList to a specific item.

        Args:
            item: The item to scroll to.
        """
        pass

    def clear(self):
        """Clear all items from the DetailedList."""
        pass

    def insert(self, index, item):
        """Insert an item at a specific index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index at which to insert the item.
            item: The item to insert.
        """
        pass

    def append(self, item):
        """Append an item to the end of the DetailedList.

        Args:
            item: The item to append.
        """
        pass

    def remove(self, item):
        """Remove an item from the DetailedList.

        Args:
            item: The item to remove.
        """
        pass

    def change_row_content(self, index, item):
        """Change the content of an item at a specific index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index of the item to change.
            item: The new item content.
        """
        pass

    def change_row_icon(self, index, icon):
        """Change the icon of an item at a specific index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index of the item to change.
            icon: The new icon for the item.
        """
        pass

    def change_row_title(self, index, title):
        """Change the title of an item at a specific index.

        Args:

        """
        pass

    def change_row_subtitle(self, index, subtitle):
        """Change the subtitle of an item at a specific index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index of the item to change.
            subtitle (str): The new subtitle for the item.
        """
        pass

    def get_selected_row(self):
        """Get the currently selected row.

        Returns:
            The selected item, or None if no item is selected.
        """
        pass

    def select_row(self, index):
        """Select a row by its index.

        Args:
            index (int): The index of the row to select.
        """
        pass

    def clear_selection(self):
        """Clear the current selection."""
        pass

    def set_on_primary_action(self, handler):
        """Set the handler for the primary action.

        Args:
            handler (callable): The function to call when the primary action is triggered.
        """
        pass

    def set_on_secondary_action(self, handler):
        """Set the handler for the secondary action.

        Args:
            handler (callable): The function to call when the secondary action is triggered.
        """
        pass

    def set_on_refresh(self, handler):
        """Set the handler for the refresh action.

            handler (callable): The function to call when the refresh action is triggered.
        """
        pass

    def set_primary_action_text(self, text):
        """Set the text for the primary action.

        Args:
            text (str): The text to display for the primary action.
```

TITLE: Clone Toga Repository (Windows)
DESCRIPTION: Clones the Toga repository from GitHub to your local machine on Windows. Replace '<your username>' with your actual GitHub username.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/how-to/contribute/code.rst#_snippet_8

LANGUAGE: doscon
CODE:
```
C:\...>git clone https://github.com/<your username>/toga.git
```

TITLE: Toga Selection Widget Reference
DESCRIPTION: API documentation for the toga.Selection class, detailing its constructor and attributes.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/selection.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
class toga.Selection:
    __init__(self, items=None, accessors=None, factory=None, **kwargs)
        Parameters:
            items (list, optional): A list of items to populate the selection with. Can be a list of strings, dictionaries, or Row objects. Defaults to None.
            accessors (str or list, optional): Specifies which attribute(s) to use for displaying items when `items` is a list of dictionaries or Row objects. If a string, it's the name of the attribute. If a list, it specifies multiple accessors for hierarchical display. Defaults to None.
            factory (toga.Factory, optional): The factory to use for creating the widget. Defaults to None.
            **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments passed to the underlying widget implementation.

    Attributes:
        value (object): The currently selected item. This will be the item from the `items` list itself, or a Row object if `items` was a list of dictionaries.
        items (toga.sources.ListSource): The source of items for the selection widget.

    Notes:
        - On macOS and Android, the font of a Selection cannot be changed.
        - On macOS, GTK, and Android, text color, background color, and text alignment of labels in a Selection cannot be changed.
        - On GTK, a Selection widget with flexible sizing expands to accommodate content but does not shrink if content width decreases.
        - On iOS, the widget size does not adapt to content or potential option list size.
```

TITLE: Toga Widget: Table (Roadmap)
DESCRIPTION: Describes the planned 'Table' widget, noting its partial implementation and the approach for mobile platforms using `toga.DetailedList`.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/background/project/roadmap.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Table:
  Status: Partially implemented
  Description: Mobile platforms don't provide a native "Table" widget; however, table-like data could be rendered by using a :class:`~toga.DetailedList`, where the title and subtitle are the "important" columns selected by the user, and selecting a row on the table navigates to a sub-page showing the full row detail.
```

TITLE: Toga Tree Widget API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides API documentation for the Toga Tree widget, including its constructor and related protocols for event handling. It details the parameters for creating a Tree and the interfaces for handling selection and activation events.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/tree.rst#_snippet_3

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Tree(headings, data=None, accessors=None, on_select=None, on_activate=None, factory=None)
  - headings: A list of strings, where each string is the heading for a column.
  - data: The data to populate the tree with. Can be a dictionary or a list of tuples.
  - accessors: A mapping from column headings to the attribute names used to retrieve data for that column.
  - on_select: A handler for the on_select event.
  - on_activate: A handler for the on_activate event.
  - factory: The widget factory to use.

toga.widgets.tree.OnSelectHandler
  - A protocol for handling the on_select event of a Tree widget.

toga.widgets.tree.OnActivateHandler
  - A protocol for handling the on_activate event of a Tree widget.
```

TITLE: Toga OptionContainer API Documentation
DESCRIPTION: Provides detailed API documentation for the Toga OptionContainer class and related components, including constructors, methods, and protocols.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/containers/optioncontainer.rst#_snippet_5

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.OptionContainer:
  __init__(content=None, id=None, style=None, **kwargs)
    content: A list of OptionContainerContentT items to populate the container.
    id: The unique ID of the widget.
    style: A Toga style object for the widget.
    **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments.

  OptionContainerContentT:
    An item of :any:`OptionContainer` content can be:
    * a 2-tuple, containing the title for the tab, and the content widget;
    * a 3-tuple, containing the title, content widget, and :any:`icon <IconContentT>`
      for the tab;
    * a 4-tuple, containing the title, content widget, :any:`icon <IconContentT>` for
      the tab, and enabled status; or
    * an :any:`toga.OptionItem` instance.

toga.OptionItem:
  __init__(text, widget, icon=None, enabled=True)
    text: The title of the option item.
    widget: The widget to display in this tab.
    icon: An icon for the tab.
    enabled: Whether the tab is enabled.

toga.widgets.optioncontainer.OptionList:
  __getitem__(index)
    Retrieves the OptionItem at the specified index.
  __delitem__(index)
    Deletes the OptionItem at the specified index.

toga.widgets.optioncontainer.OnSelectHandler:
  __call__(widget, **kwargs)
    A protocol for handling the selection of a tab in an OptionContainer.
```

TITLE: Pre-commit Checks on Windows
DESCRIPTION: Shows the command-line execution and output of pre-commit checks on a Windows system. It details the steps of adding a file and committing, followed by the results of various checks.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/how-to/contribute/code.rst#_snippet_52

LANGUAGE: doscon
CODE:
```
(.venv) C:\...>git add some\interesting_file.py
(.venv) C:\...>git commit -m "Minor change"
check toml...............................................................Passed
check yaml...............................................................Passed
check for case conflicts.................................................Passed
check docstring is first.................................................Passed
fix end of files.........................................................Passed
trim trailing whitespace.................................................Passed
ruff format..............................................................Passed
ruff check...............................................................Passed
codespell................................................................Passed
```

TITLE: Toga Icon Class Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides detailed information about the toga.Icon class, including its constructor, attributes, and methods. It clarifies the distinction between Icons and Images and outlines the expected input types for specifying an icon.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/icons.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.Icon
  __init__(self, path, **kwargs)
    path: The path to the icon file. Can be a string (absolute or relative path), a pathlib.Path object, or an instance of toga.Icon. If a relative path is provided, it is anchored to the module defining the Toga application class.

  Attributes:
    DEFAULT_ICON: A default icon instance returned when no matching icon is found.

  Usage Notes:
    - Icons are small, square, and can vary between platforms.
    - They are used as visual elements within interactive widgets (e.g., buttons, toolbars).
    - Icons are distinct from Images, which can have arbitrary sizes and are platform-independent.
    - Icon filenames should be specified without extensions; the platform determines the appropriate extension.
    - Supported formats vary by platform (PNG for Android, ICNS/PNG/BMP/ICO for iOS, ICNS/PNG/PDF for macOS, PNG/ICO/ICNS for GTK, ICO/PNG/BMP for Windows).
    - Platform-specific naming conventions (e.g., '-windows', '-linux-32') are used for icon lookup.
    - Toga attempts to resize icons to the required size and generates missing GTK variants.
    - If an icon file is found but cannot be loaded, an error is raised. Otherwise, a warning is issued and DEFAULT_ICON is returned.
```

TITLE: Icon File Format Support and Lookup
DESCRIPTION: Details the supported icon file formats for different platforms and the lookup logic Toga employs when searching for an icon. This includes platform-specific naming conventions and fallback mechanisms.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/icons.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: text
CODE:
```
Supported Formats:
- Android: PNG
- iOS: ICNS, PNG, BMP, ICO
- macOS: ICNS, PNG, PDF
- GTK: PNG, ICO, ICNS (with support for various pixel variants like 512, 256, 128, 72, 64, 32, 16px)
- Windows: ICO, PNG, BMP

Lookup Logic (Example on Windows for 'myicon'):
1. myicon-windows.ico
2. myicon.ico
3. myicon-windows.png
4. myicon.png
5. myicon-windows.bmp
6. myicon.bmp

Lookup Logic (Example on GTK for 32px variant of 'myicon'):
1. myicon-linux-32.png
2. myicon-32.png
3. myicon-linux-32.ico
4. myicon-32.ico
5. myicon-linux-32.icns
6. myicon-32.icns
7. myicon.png (fallback)
8. myicon.ico (fallback)

Note: Toga resizes found icons and generates missing GTK variants.
```

----------------------------------------

TITLE: Toga Camera and CameraDevice API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides API documentation for Toga's Camera and CameraDevice classes. Includes methods for checking and requesting camera permissions, and capturing photos. Details platform-specific permission requirements.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/hardware/camera.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.hardware.camera.Camera:
  has_permission() -> bool
    Checks if the app has permission to access the camera.

  request_permission() -> bool
    Requests permission to access the camera.

  take_photo() -> Photo
    Captures a photograph using the camera. Must be awaited.
    Returns a Photo object.

  __init__(app: App)
    Initializes the Camera API for the given Toga application.

Platform Permissions:
  iOS: Requires NSCameraUsageDescription in Info.plist.
  macOS: Requires com.apple.security.device.camera entitlement and NSCameraUsageDescription in Info.plist.
  Android: Requires android.permission.CAMERA declaration.

Note: iOS simulator cannot take photographs; use a physical device for testing.

toga.hardware.camera.CameraDevice:
  (Details for CameraDevice methods would typically be listed here, but are not provided in the source text.)
```

TITLE: TreeSource Initialization and Usage
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to initialize a TreeSource with accessors and data, access nodes and their children, find and remove rows, and insert new items. It highlights the list-like interface of TreeSource and its Node objects.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/sources/tree_source.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga.sources import TreeSource

source = TreeSource(
    accessors=["name", "height"],
    data={
        "Animals": [
            ({"name": "Numbat", "height": 0.15}, None),
            ({"name": "Thylacine", "height": 0.6}, None),
        ],
        "Plants": [
            ({"name": "Woollybush", "height": 2.4}, None),
            ({"name": "Boronia", "height": 0.9}, None),
        ],
    }
)

# Get the Animal group in the source.
# The Animal group won't have a "height" attribute.
group = source[0]
print(f"Group's name is {group.name}")

# Get the second item in the animal group
animal = group[1]
print(f"Animals's name is {animal.name}; it is {animal.height}m tall.")

# Find an animal with a name of "Thylacine"
row = source.find(parent=source[0], {"name": "Thylacine"})

# Remove that row from the data. Even though "Thylacine" isn't a root node,
# remove will find it and remove it from the list of animals.
source.remove(row)

# Insert a new item at the start of the list of animals.
group.insert(0, {"name": "Bettong", "height": 0.35})

# Insert a new root item in the middle of the list of root nodes
source.insert(1, {"name": "Minerals"})
```

TITLE: Toga Application Header and Layout Styles
DESCRIPTION: Provides CSS styles for the Toga application header, including brand logo, navigation menu, and responsiveness. It also defines styles for the main window and container layout, ensuring proper overflow and flexbox behavior.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/examples/tutorial0/sandbox.html#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: css
CODE:
```
header.toga {
  background-color: var(--sl-color-primary-200);
  color: var(--sl-color-neutral-900);
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: row;
  align-items: center;
}
header.toga a.brand {
  margin: 8px 1em;
}
header.toga a.brand img {
  width: 32px;
}
header.toga nav {
  display: inline-block;
}
header.toga nav.menubar {
  flex-grow: 1;
  font-family: var(--sl-font-sans);
  font-size: var(--sl-font-size-large);
}
header.toga nav.menubar:last-of-type {
  flex-grow: 0;
  padding-right: 1em;
}
header.toga nav.menubar .menu {
  color: var(--sl-color-neutral-700);
  padding: 0.5em;
}
header.toga nav.menubar .menu:hover {
  background-color: var(--sl-color-primary-300);
  color: var(--sl-color-primary-600);
}
header.toga nav.menubar .app {
  color: var(--sl-color-neutral-900);
  font-weight: var(--sl-font-weight-bold);
}
main.toga.window {
  overflow: hidden;
  display: flex;
}
main.toga.window > .container {
  flex-grow: 1 !important;
}
.toga .container {
  display: flex;
}
```

TITLE: Toga TimeInput API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides the API documentation for the Toga TimeInput widget, including its constructor and supported properties.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/timeinput.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.TimeInput:
  __init__(id=None, style=None, on_change=None, enabled=True, **kwargs)
    Initializes a TimeInput widget.
    Parameters:
      id (str, optional): A unique identifier for the widget.
      style (toga.Style, optional): Styling for the widget.
      on_change (callable, optional): A function to call when the time value changes.
      enabled (bool, optional): Whether the widget is enabled (defaults to True).
      **kwargs: Additional keyword arguments.

  Properties:
    value (datetime.time): The currently selected time. Can accept datetime.time, datetime.datetime, or ISO8601 formatted strings.
    enabled (bool): Whether the widget is enabled.

Notes:
  - Supports hours, minutes, and seconds. Microseconds are always zero.
  - On Android and iOS, seconds are also zeroed, and second components of min/max values are ignored.
  - Accepts datetime.datetime and ISO8601 strings for the `value` property.

Related Protocols:
  toga.widgets.timeinput.OnChangeHandler: Defines the signature for the on_change callback.
```

TITLE: Toga ImageView API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides the API documentation for the toga.ImageView class, detailing its constructor and behavior.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/imageview.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
toga.ImageView(image: toga.Image | None = None, **kwargs)
  A widget that displays an image.

  Parameters:
    image: The image to display. Defaults to None.

  Notes:
    - An ImageView is not an interactive element.
    - Default size is the image size or 0x0 if image is None.
    - Explicit width/height fixes size in that axis, other axis determined by aspect ratio.
    - Explicit width AND height scales image without preserving aspect ratio.
    - flex=1 allows expansion/contraction along the main axis based on flex allocation.
      - If cross axis size is unspecified, it's determined by the image's aspect ratio.
      - If cross axis has an explicit size, the image scales to fill space while preserving aspect ratio.
```

TITLE: Toga Widget Interface Implementation
DESCRIPTION: This section describes the process of implementing platform-native widgets in Toga. It references documentation for supported widgets and suggests looking for `factory.not_implemented()` calls for missing widgets.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/how-to/contribute/code.rst#_snippet_14

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Implement a platform native widget

If the core library already specifies an interface for a widget, but the widget isn't implemented on your platform of choice, implement that interface.

Dependencies:
- Toga core library
- Platform-specific native APIs

Process:
1. Identify missing widgets using the supported widgets by platform table or by looking for `factory.not_implemented()` calls.
2. Implement the widget interface for the target platform.
3. Write tests for the new core API and add a probe for the backend if extending an existing widget.
```

TITLE: Creating and Modifying OptionContainer Tabs (Tuple Format)
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to create an OptionContainer with initial tabs using tuples, and how to add more tabs dynamically. Each tab can have a label, content, and an optional icon.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/containers/optioncontainer.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

pizza = toga.Box()
pasta = toga.Box()

# Create 2 initial tabs; one with an icon, and one without.
container = toga.OptionContainer(
    content=[("Pizza", pizza), ("Pasta", pasta, toga.Icon("pasta"))]
)

# Add another tab of content, without an icon.
salad = toga.Box()
container.content.append("Salad", salad)

# Add another tab of content, with an icon
icecream = toga.Box()
container.content.append("Ice Cream", icecream, toga.Icon("icecream"))
```

TITLE: Toga Widget Layout Mechanism Overview
DESCRIPTION: Explains how Toga manages widget positioning and sizing using a style engine, intrinsic sizes, and a tree-like container structure.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/how-to/topics/layout.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
Toga Layout System:
  - Style Engine:
      - Purpose: Determines widget display position and size.
      - Default: Pack (built-in style engine).
      - Usage: Every widget has a style object used for layout.
  - Intrinsic Size:
      - Definition: Widget size reported by underlying GUI library.
      - Properties: Width and height; each dimension can be fixed or minimum.
      - Recalculation: "Rehinting" re-calculates intrinsic size and invalidates layout upon property changes (e.g., text, font).
  - Widget Tree Structure:
      - Root: Container widget.
      - Hierarchy: Widgets keep reference to their root container.
      - Layout Process: Layout engine asks container's style object to lay out its contents, calculating size/position for all widgets in the tree.
  - Containers:
      - Window Container: Represents total viewable area.
      - Sub-containers: Established by widgets with "Container" in their name; refresh of parent container refreshes sub-containers.
```

TITLE: Create and Populate Toga Box
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to create a Toga Box widget and add child widgets to it, both during initialization and after construction.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/containers/box.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga

# Create an empty Box and add children later
box1 = toga.Box()
label1 = toga.Label('Hello')
label2 = toga.Label('World')
box1.add(label1)
box1.add(label2)

# Create a Box with children specified at initialization
label3 = toga.Label('Hello')
label4 = toga.Label('World')
box2 = toga.Box(children=[label3, label4])
```

TITLE: Manipulating Toga Commands
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates how to interact with Toga's command system. This includes removing the default Preferences command, customizing the text of the About command, and adding a new custom Open command with a specified action.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/command.rst#_snippet_3

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
del self.commands[toga.Command.PREFERENCES]

self.commands[toga.Command.ABOUT].text = "I'm Customized!!"

custom_open = toga.Command.standard(
    self,
    toga.Command.OPEN,
    action=self.custom_open
)

self.commands.add(custom_open)
```

TITLE: Toga Key Modifiers and Combinations
DESCRIPTION: Demonstrates the use of Toga's generic modifier constants (MOD_1, MOD_2, MOD_3) and the '+' operator to create key combinations for keyboard shortcuts. It shows how to represent single keys, shifted keys, and keys combined with platform-specific modifiers.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/keys.rst#_snippet_0

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
from toga import Key

just_an_a = Key.A
shift_a = Key.SHIFT + Key.A
# Windows/Linux - Control-Shift-A:
# macOS - Command-Shift-A:
modified_shift_a = Key.MOD_1 + Key.SHIFT + Key.A
```

TITLE: Creating Font Instances for APIs
DESCRIPTION: Shows how to create explicit Font instances, which are required by certain Toga APIs like Canvas.write_text. It demonstrates specifying font family, size, and weight.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/fonts.rst#_snippet_2

LANGUAGE: python
CODE:
```
import toga
from toga.style.pack import BOLD

# Obtain a 14 point Serif bold font instance
my_font = toga.Font(SERIF, 14, weight=BOLD)

# Use the font to write on a canvas.
canvas = toga.Canvas()
canvas.context.write_text("Hello", font=my_font)
```

TITLE: Toga Image Class Reference
DESCRIPTION: API documentation for the toga.Image class, detailing its constructor and accepted image content types.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/resources/images.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
autoclass :: toga.Image

ImageContentT:
    When specifying content for an Image, you can provide:
    * a string specifying an absolute or relative path to a file in a known image format;
    * an absolute or relative pathlib.Path object describing a file in a known image format;
    * a "blob of bytes" data type (bytes, bytearray, or memoryview) containing raw image data in a known image format;
    * an instance of toga.Image;
    * if Pillow is installed, an instance of PIL.Image.Image;
    * an image of a class registered via an image format plugin (or a subclass of such a class); or
    * an instance of the native platform image representation.

    If a relative path is provided, it will be anchored relative to the module that defines your Toga application class.
```

TITLE: Toga DateInput API Reference
DESCRIPTION: Provides details on the toga.DateInput class, including its constructor and supported properties.

SOURCE: https://github.com/beeware/toga/blob/main/docs/reference/api/widgets/dateinput.rst#_snippet_1

LANGUAGE: APIDOC
CODE:
```
class toga.DateInput:
    """A widget to select a calendar date."""

    def __init__(self, id=None, style=None, on_change=None, factory=None, **kwargs):
        """Initialize DateInput.

        Args:
            id (str): A string identifier for this widget.
            style (:class:`toga.Style`): An object to hold the style for this widget.
            on_change (callable): A function that will be called when the date changes.
            factory (:module:`toga.platform`): A factory to create the native widget.
            **kwargs: Dict of properties to be set on the widget.
        """
        pass

    # Properties:
    # - value: The currently selected date (datetime.date).
    # - enabled: Whether the widget is enabled.
    # - visible: Whether the widget is visible.
    # - style: The style of the widget.
    # - on_change: The callback function for date changes.

    # Supported date formats for the 'value' property:
    # - datetime.date
    # - datetime.datetime (date portion will be extracted)
    # - str (ISO8601 format, e.g., "2023-12-25")

    # Limitations:
    # - Years supported: 1800 to 8999.
    # - On iOS, color and background color styling are ignored.

protocol toga.widgets.dateinput.OnChangeHandler:
    """The handler for the DateInput's on_change event."""

    def __call__(self, widget):
        """Called when the date in the DateInput widget has changed.

        Args:
            widget (:class:`toga.DateInput`): The widget that triggered the event.
        """
        pass

```
